﻿﻿﻿﻿# Grant Pathway — ADR Consequences Traceability

**Tier:** 2 — Check if relevant to the task
**Volatility:** Medium
**Update when:** A GAP item is resolved or a phase gate is passed

**Purpose:** Maps every ADR consequence to a specific implementation task. A gap in this table means a consequence has no covering task — it will not be implemented unless a task is added. This is the single place to see whether the full spec is covered.

## Status symbols — read this before trusting any row

**Added 2026-07-30 (Phase 5 restructure item C2, approved by WJ).** Until today a single ✅ in this table meant **two different things**, and a signed phase gate depended on the difference.

| Symbol       | Meaning                                                                                                                                                         | Do not read it as                                       |
| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
| ✅           | **Built.** The code, configuration or artefact exists. The ADR consequence is genuinely discharged.                                                             | —                                                       |
| 📋           | **Tasked only — NOT built.** A step exists in `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` and nothing more. The consequence is **not** discharged and the code does **not** exist. | Do not read as done. This is a promise, not an outcome. |
| ➖           | **Accepted deviation.** Consciously not doing it, with the decision and residual risk recorded in the row.                                                      | —                                                       |
| ⚠️           | **Gap.** No covering task at all.                                                                                                                               | —                                                       |
| 🔵 / Partial | Some parts built, others tasked or deferred. The row says which.                                                                                                | —                                                       |

**Why this exists.** GAP-24's status read `✅ 2026-06-16 — disclaimer wording fix step added to P5.3`, meaning _a task was written_. Two rows away, GAP-26's `✅ Resolved 2026-06-15 (commit 372d95b)` meant _the code exists_. Same column, same symbol, opposite claims. The consequence was not theoretical: the **Phase 4 → Phase 5 gate sign-off** (2026-05-22, signed WJ 2026-06-17) reads "GAP-05/12/17/21/22/23/24/25/26 resolved" — a statement that was true as written and false as read. See the annotation on that gate row.

**Sixteen rows were reclassified from ✅ to 📋 on 2026-07-30** — ten in the Gaps register (GAP-03, 05, 12, 17, 21, 22, 23, 25, 28, 31) and six in the ADR consequence tables (ADR-ARCH-002, ADR-ARCH-003, ADR-ARCH-005, ADR-OPS-005, ADR-SEC-002, ADR-SEC-003). **The ADR-table half matters more than the register half:** those are the rows a future session reads when performing the mandatory ADR-consequences check in `AGENTS.md` §2, so a ✅ there would have told it the consequence was discharged when the code did not exist.

**How to use:**

- Before starting any phase, scan for ⚠️ Gap **and 📋 rows**. Every gap must be resolved (task added or N/A justified) before phase work begins, and every 📋 is unbuilt work still owed.
- When a new task is added to cover a gap, update the Task column and set the status to **📋** — not ✅.
- **When the code actually lands, change 📋 → ✅ and say what was built.** This is the step that was missing, and it is why a tick came to mean two things.
- Task-level progress still lives in `IMPLEMENTATION-STATUS.md`; this table records whether the ADR consequence itself is discharged.
- **These five symbols are the vocabulary; the [Gaps register](#gaps-register) additionally shows each one as a colour disc** (🔴 🟡 🔵 ⚪ 🟢) in a dedicated Status column, added 2026-08-06 so the register can be triaged by eye. The symbols and the discs mean the same five things — the discs exist because 📋 was not visually alarming enough for what it means. **The ADR consequence tables below still use the symbols alone**, and both halves must be kept in step: on 2026-08-06, `GAP-01`, `GAP-02` and `GAP-04` read ⚠️ here and ✅ in the register, all three having been built on 2026-05-21.

| 2.76    | 2026-08-15 | Rapidglobe Ltd | **`GAP-100` raised and fixed same day — production Supabase auth was pointing at a domain owned by somebody else.** Found during `P5.4`'s Supabase walkthrough. `grant-pathway-prod`'s Site URL and its only allowlisted callback both pointed at `https://grant-pathway.vercel.app`, which serves **"Grant Pathway – Find Grants for Your Canadian Business"** — an unrelated product sharing the name, and not a domain on this Vercel project. **Three failures at once:** our real host was not allowlisted (so every production registration would have fallen back to Site URL and delivered the verification token to the third party); password reset's `?next=reset` query string cannot match an exact-match entry (Supabase's docs confirm `*` stops at `.`/`/`, `**` does not); and neither `grantpathway.org.uk` nor `www.` was listed, guaranteeing failure at the `P5.6` cutover. The reset failure was **silent by design** — `requestPasswordReset` is deliberately fire-and-forget for enumeration resistance (`AC-FR-05-02`), so a rejected redirect reaches neither user nor Sentry. **Severity assessed both ways:** the attack variant is **blocked** (Next.js Server Actions compare `Origin` to `Host` and abort on mismatch — verified in the bundled `data-security.md`), but the **normal-use** variant needed no attacker. **Zero actual exposure** — production has never had a real user and DNS has never been cut over. Fixed by WJ the same day and screenshot-verified: Site URL → the real pre-launch host, third-party entry deleted, three `/auth/callback**` entries added. | 2.74    | 2026-08-15 | Rapidglobe Ltd | **`P5.2` (Security) executed. `GAP-17` and `GAP-48` both close on evidence; `GAP-96`–`GAP-99` raised, three of them fixed the same day.** Full artefact: `docs/legal/security-review-2026-08-15.md`. **`GAP-17` (cross-user RLS, open since 2026-06-16) — 22/22 assertions pass.** Two throwaway confirmed users, every attack run from an anon-key client carrying the attacker's real JWT rather than the service role, which bypasses RLS and would have made every assertion pass regardless. Six positive controls included deliberately, because a policy that denies everyone passes a negative-only test. Includes the `WITH CHECK` forged-insert case — rejected. **`GAP-48` (storage policies) — 7/7 pass, and this is the first time in the project's history those policies have been _observed_ to grant.** Their own migration said so: applying cleanly proves the SQL executes, not that the predicate works, and the originals applied cleanly then denied everything for eleven weeks. ⚠️ **A fixture defect in the test harness had to be found first and it is the more instructive result:** the first run used a `text/plain` upload, which the bucket's `allowed_mime_types` rejects **before RLS is consulted**, so the deny assertions were passing for the wrong reason and proved nothing. A security test that passes for the wrong reason is worse than one that fails. **New gaps:** `GAP-96` (Medium — CSP had no `base-uri` or `form-action`, the two directives that do **not** fall back to `default-src`, so there was no protection on either axis rather than the inherited `'self'` a reader would assume) and `GAP-97` (Low — no explicit `object-src`) both fixed in `middleware.ts`; `GAP-98` (Low — `X-Powered-By` disclosure, confirmed live) fixed via `poweredByHeader: false`. `GAP-99` (Low–Medium — no app-level rate limit on `signIn`/`registerUser`/`requestPasswordReset`) **assessed and deliberately not fixed**: too tight a sign-in limit locks a charity out of its own grant application, which is a product judgement, not a security one; the action owed at `P5.4` is to read and record the prod dashboard's actual auth limits, because the gap is that nobody has looked. **Also confirmed:** `npm audit` 0 vulnerabilities at every severity; no secret value from `.env.local` appears in any tracked file; server-only keys unreachable from client code; Sentry `beforeSend` scrubbing live on all three runtimes with the `instrumentation-client.ts` wiring checked rather than assumed; CSP clean across seven routes; Supabase session expiry exactly 3600s against the 60-minute app timeout. ⚠️ **Every live check ran against `grant-pathway-dev`** — RLS, storage and session expiry prove dev only and must be re-confirmed at `P5.4`/`P5.5`. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, 280 tests, clean `next build`. Guarded by new `user_profiles.last_inactivity_warned_at` (migration `20260805000000`). | 2.13    | 2026-07-29 | Rapidglobe Ltd | **GAP-11 closed as an accepted deviation (Opus audit M6). Decision: WJ.** The previous 🔴 BLOCKED status was wrong on both counts. Branch protection was never blocked on a GitHub Pro upgrade — classic protection is live on `master` with all three CI contexts required, `strict` mode on, and force-pushes and deletion blocked. And the recorded workaround, "team enforces PR review manually," described a process that neither happens nor is required: there is no team and no PR-review requirement. The branch is `master`, not `main`, as the old rows said. What is now explicitly accepted is that `enforce_admins` stays `false`. Enabling it was considered and rejected on a concrete mechanical ground: required status checks are evaluated against the commit being pushed, but the checks only run after the push, so a direct push can never satisfy them — admin enforcement would block all direct pushes and force a PR workflow in which the sole developer approves their own PRs, contradicting `AGENTS.md` §5 for no added reviewer. Residual risk (an unreviewed commit can reach production, and Vercel deploys independently of CI) and the mitigations relied on instead are recorded in the GAP-11 row. Revisit if a second contributor joins. Also deleted the dead repository ruleset "Protect Master" (id `16804175`, `enforcement: disabled`, created 2026-05-24 — the abandoned attempt this GAP recorded as blocked) so it cannot be mistaken for live protection. `ADR-OPS-002` and `ADR-STACK-005` consequence rows updated to ✅ and their stale `main`/GitHub-Pro wording corrected.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

## Status key

| Symbol | Meaning                                                                          |
| ------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ✅     | Covered — mapped to a specific named task                                        |
| ⚠️     | Gap — no task covers this; must be resolved before phase begins                  |
| 🔵     | Deferred — correctly planned for a later phase                                   |
| ➖     | N/A — documentation reference, self-referential, or superseded by a PDR decision |

## Gaps register

**⚠️ Do not run `prettier --write` on this file.** Found 2026-08-12: prettier 3.9.6 has a reproducible bug where reformatting this table — once any cell is added or edited near the current longest-row size (~6,000+ characters) — corrupts the *entire* table, not just the changed row: words get truncated by one character, punctuation is dropped, and markup gets duplicated, even on rows far from the edit. Confirmed on a scratch copy before it reached the committed file; `prettier --check` (read-only) does not corrupt anything and is safe to run. Edit new rows by hand, matching the existing column-padding style as closely as is practical, and leave the file un-formatted by `--write` until this is root-caused (or the table is restructured to avoid the failure) — needs a WJ decision on how to resolve permanently, not a workaround baked in silently.

Every gap raised against this project, **sorted by number and colour-coded** (both introduced 2026-08-06 at WJ's request). This table is the triage list: read the Status column and nothing else if you are in a hurry.

| Status         | Meaning                                                                        | What to do with it                                                                                     |
| -------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| 🔴 No task     | No covering task exists anywhere. Nobody is going to do this.                  | **Schedule it or justify closing it.** Nothing else will surface it.                                   |
| 🟡 Tasked only | A step exists in `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` and **the code does not exist**.     | **Treat as outstanding work.** This is the category that has been misread before — see the note below. |
| 🔵 Partial     | Some of it is built; the row says which part is not, and why.                  | Read the row. Usually one named check or deployment step away from 🟢.                                 |
| ⚪ Accepted    | Consciously not doing it. The decision and the residual risk are in the row.   | Nothing, unless the reasoning has expired.                                                             |
| 🟢 Built       | The code, configuration or artefact exists. The ADR consequence is discharged. | Nothing.                                                                                               |

**Why the colours, and why 🟡 is the point of them.** The five categories already existed as ✅ 📋 🔵 ➖ ⚠️ (see the key at the top of this document, added 2026-07-30). What they lacked was contrast: 📋 is a beige clipboard, so the single most dangerous category — **work that is promised but does not exist** — looked the mildest thing in the table. That is precisely the misreading that let the Phase 4 → Phase 5 gate sign off nine gaps as "resolved" when only one had been built. The disc makes it impossible to skim past. Each disc is paired with a word rather than standing alone, because colour on its own is not an accessible signal and this project holds itself to that standard elsewhere (`NFR-06`).

**Sorted numerically as of 2026-08-06.** The table had drifted into insertion order (…31, 30, 35, 38, 37, 36, 33, 39…), which made it impossible to see at a glance which numbers were in use — a contributing condition to `GAP-32` sitting outside this register for four weeks and to two parallel sessions each issuing `GAP-39` and `GAP-40` on the same day. **Keep it sorted, and add new gaps in numeric position.** All numbers 01–**89** are now present and each appears exactly once. **`GAP-89` is the highest number issued** — allocate the next one from here, and per the collision below, **check it against a freshly fetched `origin/master` first**, not against whatever tree you happen to be in.

                                                                              | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-13.** Built as a CSS-only overlay (`components/mobile-viewport-banner.tsx`, `flex md:hidden`, wired into `app/layout.tsx` alongside a `hidden md:flex` wrapper around the rest of the app) — Tailwind's default `md` breakpoint is exactly 768px in this project (confirmed via `app/globals.css`, no custom `--breakpoint` override), so no custom media query, `matchMedia`, or JavaScript was needed, and there is no hydration-mismatch risk. Copy and icon decided fresh (no prior spec existed): heading "Please use a desktop or laptop", body explains the concrete reason (Step 4's side-by-side guidelines/draft layout) rather than a bare refusal, per `docs/Business Design/tone-and-voice-guide.md`'s Plain English/Encouraging pillars — recorded as `DDR-CS-007`. `MonitorSmartphone` (`lucide-react`) chosen as the icon, no existing usage to conflict with. **Live-verified**, on both a public route and mid-application on an authenticated Step 4 page: banner shown and app `display:none` at 767px and 375px; banner hidden and app fully interactive at the exact 768px boundary, 800px and 1280px (no visual regression to the existing header/footer flex stacking). **Dialog-portal edge case checked, not fully hardened by design:** Base UI's `Dialog` portals to `document.body` outside the new wrapper `div`, so `z-[9999]` was chosen to sit well above the dialog's `z-50` — confirmed live via a simulated portaled overlay that the banner still wins `document.elementFromPoint()` at screen centre below 768px. A keyboard-only user could in principle still tab into a hidden dialog's controls without seeing them; left as documented residual risk rather than adding `inert`/focus-trap JS, since sub-768px keyboard/AT testing is already out of scope per `accessibility-test-plan.md` (line 46) and `GAP-05` is Low severity precisely because "block the UI" is the decided mitigation, not a fully-hardened one. 3 new component tests (`__tests__/mobile-viewport-banner.test.tsx`); `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0` and all 269 tests pass. **Closes `accessibility-test-plan.md`'s last open dependency** — AC-11's and AC-15's deferred small-viewport sub-checks re-scoped and closed via a live sweep of the previously-untested 768–1023px functional band (see `ADR-TRACEABILITY.md`'s Document History and `accessibility-test-plan.md`'s AC-11/AC-15 addenda for the full account); the plan moves 🔴 → 🟢 in `TEST-DASHBOARD.md` for the first time.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |

| ⚪ Accepted    | GAP-11 | ADR-OPS-002, ADR-STACK-005                                                                                                                                                                                  | GitHub branch protection rules on `master` — configured, but admins are exempt                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | Medium   | Closed 2026-07-29 — accepted deviation                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | ➖ **Closed as an accepted deviation, 2026-07-29 (Opus audit M6). Decision: WJ.** The old status was wrong twice over. Protection was **not** blocked on a GitHub Pro upgrade — classic branch protection is live on `master`, requiring the three CI contexts (`lint-and-typecheck`, `test`, `validate-migrations`), with `strict` mode on and force-pushes and branch deletion both blocked. And the stated workaround, "team enforces PR review manually," described a process that neither happens nor is required: there is no team and no PR-review requirement. The branch is also `master`, not `main`. **What is accepted:** `enforce_admins` remains `false`, so the sole contributor bypasses the required checks on every direct push. Enabling it was considered and rejected — required status checks are evaluated on the commit being pushed, but the checks only run _after_ the push, so a direct push can never satisfy them; admin enforcement would therefore block all direct pushes to `master` and force a pull-request workflow. For a single developer that means self-approving every PR, which adds process without adding a reviewer, and it would contradict `AGENTS.md` §5's instruction to push to `origin master` immediately after committing. **Residual risk:** nothing prevents an unreviewed commit reaching production, and CI is not a gate (see the `DEPLOYMENT-CHECKLIST.md` note on Vercel deploying independently of CI). Mitigations relied on instead: the pre-deploy checklist, Vercel instant rollback, `AI_ENABLED` as an AI kill-switch, and CI now being genuinely green so a real failure is visible. Revisit if a second contributor joins — at that point admin enforcement plus PR review becomes worthwhile rather than ceremonial. ✅ **RE-AFFIRMED AND CLOSED 2026-08-16 (WJ), discharging `ADR-OPS-002` consequence 1** — which had been open since 2026-04-21 and was the last undecided item in `P5.4`. The 2026-07-29 reasoning holds, and **one point sharpened it into a decision: branch protection is not a production gate on this project.** Vercel deploys from `master` on push, independently of CI, so protection governs what enters the branch and not what reaches users — meaning `enforce_admins = true` would block a *merge* on a red run while doing nothing about a *deployment*. **The control actually relied on is running `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, the full suite and a clean `next build` before pushing**, which was done ahead of every code push on 2026-08-16. **Two revisit triggers recorded rather than left implicit:** a second contributor joining, and any decision to gate deployment properly — the latter being a change to **Vercel**, not GitHub, and scoped to `P5.6`. Full reasoning in `ADR-OPS-002`'s Consequences. Also actioned: the dead repository ruleset "Protect Master" (id `16804175`, `enforcement: disabled`, created 2026-05-24 — the abandoned first attempt this GAP recorded as blocked) was **deleted**, so it can no longer be mistaken for live protection. |

| 🟢 Built | GAP-17 | ADR-SEC-002                                                                                                                                                                                                 | RLS cross-user access test never tasked                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Medium   | Add to P5.2                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-15 — executed under `P5.2`, 22/22 assertions passed.** Method and full results in `docs/legal/security-review-2026-08-15.md` §6. Two throwaway confirmed users; A's data seeded with the service role so seeding could not itself be blocked by the policies under test; **every attack run from an anon-key client carrying B's real JWT** — never the service role, which bypasses RLS and would have made every assertion pass regardless. **Six positive controls were included deliberately**, because a policy that denies everyone passes a negative-only test — which is exactly how `GAP-48`'s storage policies denied every request for eleven weeks unnoticed. Coverage: B cannot SELECT (6 tables), UPDATE (5) or DELETE (4) any of A's rows; **B cannot INSERT a row owned by A** — the `WITH CHECK` class of bug, where reads are correctly hidden but a row can still be created under another user's id — rejected; and A's data was confirmed unmodified after every attempt. Tables: `applications`, `application_items`, `application_guidelines`, `charity_profiles`, `user_profiles`, `ai_usage_log`. ⚠️ **Proves `grant-pathway-dev` only** — prod is not linked until `P5.4`; re-confirm there. _Previously: 📋 2026-06-16 — explicit cross-user RLS test step added to P5.2 in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md_ `docs/legal/dependency-licences-2026-07-30.md`. **Conclusion: no licence in the dependency tree prevents a closed-source, proprietary, hosted service.** All 25 direct production dependencies are permissive (MIT / Apache-2.0 / ISC / BSD-2-Clause); **no AGPL, SSPL or BUSL anywhere across 889 installed packages** — the finding that matters most here, since AGPL-3.0 treats network use as distribution and would otherwise require offering source to every user; and no package is unlicensed. Four production-reachable packages carry real conditions, none a problem under the current model: `@sentry/cli` (FSL-1.1-MIT, build-time only, non-compete clause dormant), `sharp`'s native binaries (LGPL-3.0-or-later, separately invoked, nothing distributed), `jszip` (dual-licensed — **MIT elected**, and it genuinely ships, underpinning Word export and `.docx` extraction), `caniuse-lite` (CC-BY-4.0 data, build-time). The conclusion rests on Grant Pathway remaining a **hosted service**; the review names exactly what to re-check if that changes. **Note for the record: this status cell was entirely empty from 2026-05-20 until 2026-07-30** — no tick, no symbol, no note — the only such row in the register, which is how a task nobody had started stayed invisible for ten weeks. Raised independently as audit finding **L8**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |

there are eleven page routes.** Built as four rather than eleven deliberately: Next.js wraps "page.js and any children below" in the boundary and **there is no nested layout anywhere beneath these points**, so a boundary at `applications/` replaces exactly the region seven separate ones under it would — eleven identical three-line files would be duplication with no behavioural difference. A nearer `loading.tsx` can be added later if a route needs a tailored skeleton; it takes precedence automatically. **Placement is the part worth remembering:** these sit per-route rather than once at `app/(authenticated)/loading.tsx`, which is the obvious single-file answer and would have done **nothing** — that layout is `async` and calls `supabase.auth.getUser()`, and the `loading.js` docs state that a layout reading runtime data gets no fallback, with navigation blocking until it finishes. **Verified live:** a `MutationObserver` on a real signed-in session recorded the skeleton genuinely mounting on client-side navigation to `/dashboard`, `/profile` and `/applications/[id]/step/4` (the last proving the `applications/` boundary serves nested children); `/account` and `/applications/new` confirmed via the fallback appearing in streamed server HTML. **`/account/delete` emits no fallback and correctly so** — its page is a _synchronous_ server component with no data access, so nothing suspends. Checked per-route rather than assumed, because an absent fallback can mean "no boundary" or "nothing to wait for". `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, 280 tests, clean `next build`. _Previously: 📋 Plan/ROLLBACK-PROCEDURE.md`.** Written as an operational document rather than a checklist bullet, and deliberately **written for a solo non-developer reading it during an incident**, not before one. Opens with a symptom-to-section table, because the first question under pressure is "which kind of problem is this?" **It covers four failure modes the original four-step bullet did not**, all of them observed or established during `P5.4`: (1) **check before rolling back** — if `/api/health` returns 200 the app is healthy and a rollback changes something that is not broken; (2) **read-only or failing saves is almost certainly the Supabase spend cap**, for which a code rollback does nothing, and which gives **no warning** before it bites (`ADR-DATA-005`); (3) **registration and password-reset failures are configuration, not code** — with the three causes actually found this session (`GAP-100` redirect URLs, `GAP-101` SMTP, Resend domain verification) and the warning that reset failures are **silent by design** under `AC-FR-05-02`, so Resend's log is the only place they appear; (4) **data incidents** — do not open the SQL editor (`ADR-DATA-004`), note that a restore replaces the whole database and that **backups exclude Storage objects**. Also records the case the original bullet missed entirely: **rolling back code does not roll back a migration.** The four original steps (one-click revert, business-hours deployment, verify the fix before redeploying, record it in `CHANGELOG.md`) are all retained. _Previously: 📋 2026-06-16 — rollback procedure step added to P5.4 in IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md (Vercel one-click revert, business-hours | 🟢 Built       | GAP-36 | PDR-UI-008                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `tt-charity-lookup` (Profile page, Charity Commission search input) flashed open for roughly two milliseconds on first hover (or Tab focus), then disappeared, instead of staying open. Found 2026-07-25 by WJ live-testing HT-02 of the simplified (post-v3.0) tooltip behaviour. Four hypotheses tested live and ruled out before the real cause was found: browser-extension interference (Incognito across Chrome, Perplexity Comet, Edge), `type="search"`'s native decoration (reverted to `type="text"`, still flashed), Base UI's `InputPrimitive` specifically (swapped for a plain native `<input>`, still flashed per WJ's manual test), and a `side="top"` viewport-edge collision (forced `side="bottom"`, still flashed — just repositioned below the field). WJ's live test of a _second_ diagnostic tooltip on the same page (Charity name field) also flashed, ruling out "specific to the lookup input" entirely and narrowing it to something page/component-wide. **Root-caused via direct live debugging** (author drove WJ's own authenticated Chrome session with his permission, no credentials entered) using a `MutationObserver` + dispatched pointer/focus events: `type` and `name` attributes on `#charity-lookup` were being toggled repeatedly during the hover window. Confirmed via a controlled A/B on the same live page: an identical Base UI `Input` elsewhere on the page with **no** tooltip showed zero attribute churn under the same event sequence — the churn only happens on the tooltip-wrapped input. Root cause: Base UI's `Tooltip.Trigger`, composing its own props onto a polymorphic `render` target, applies button-oriented default props (`type`, `name`) — sensible for a `<button>` trigger, but setting `type` on a genuine `<input>` forces Chromium to reinitialise the native widget, blurring it instantly and closing the tooltip.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Medium   | Added a `wrapInStableSpan` (+ `spanClassName`) option to `components/contextual-tooltip.tsx`, reusing the existing focusable-span-wrapper mechanism (previously only used for the hover-disabled case) so Base UI composes its props onto a stable `<span>` instead of directly onto the input. Applied to `tt-charity-lookup`; the earlier disproven `type`/`side` experiments and the temporary diagnostic tooltip were reverted/removed. Buttons, links, and plain text elements are unaffected (no `type`-dependent native behaviour) and don't need this. Type-check/lint/95 tests pass. Confirmed fixed live on the deployed build (`cd8ce83`) — zero attribute mutations on `#charity-lookup` under a scripted hover/focus event sequence (versus repeated churn before the fix), and a screenshot confirms the tooltip stays visibly open.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | ✅ Resolved 2026-07-25 — confirmed live by the author driving WJ's session; WJ's own manual re-test still a good final sanity check.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

Both are genuine application questions that a funder assesses on; both happen to take a short answer (a number, a duration). `lib/prompts.ts`'s "questions" rule excludes "data-entry fields (name, address, phone, email, website, charity number, dates, postcode)" and the TABLE FORMAT rule skips "Short numerical field" — and the **only** carve-out from either is the BUDGET/COST exception, added twice on 2026-07-27 — once for MK Community Foundation's general exclusion rule, once for Idlewild's table-format skip-list (`guideline-capability-matrix-test-plan.md` Defect Log #1). Six-month and twelve-month outcomes are exactly that case. **Lower severity than `GAP-39` because nothing is lost** — the applicant still sees the full text of both asks and can answer both in one box (the real application did) — but a funder with per-question word limits, or a form where the two asks diverge more sharply, would be materially misrepresented. Distinct from `GAP-39`: that one is a rule with an inadequate exception, this one is an adequate rule the model did not follow.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Medium   | ⚠️ **Not yet tasked** — same 2026-08-06 hold as `GAP-39`. His wife deliberately laid out her §4b answer (Q6, 215 words) with blank lines, a hyphen-bulleted list of facilities, a "Funding to Date" block and a worked calculation; the downloaded `.docx` rendered all of it as one continuous run of prose. **The data is not lost anywhere else** — the `<Textarea>` stores `\n` intact, the Step 5 preview renders it correctly via `whitespace-pre-wrap` (`application-step5-approve.tsx`), and the `format=txt` export is unaffected because it joins an array of lines. **The defect is confined to two lines of the Word generator**: `app/api/export/[applicationId]/route.ts` puts the entire answer body into a **single** `TextRun` (once in the `assembled_draft` branch, once in the per-answer fallback branch), and the `docx` library ignores `\n` inside a run — a documented footgun of that library, not a subtle interaction. Applies to every narrative field including the budget narrative (§5a), which is an ordinary `Textarea`; the two governance `£` inputs are single numbers and unaffected. **Note the test that should have caught it: `regression-test-plan.md` RT-09 passed on 2026-07-28 verifying "no corrupted or missing content" — with single-paragraph answers, which never exercised the bug.**                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | High     | ⚠️ **Not yet tasked** — WJ asked for it to be logged on 2026-08-06 alongside `GAP-39`/`GAP-40`. | 🟢 Built       | GAP-43 | FR-18, AC-FR-18-03, PRD §7 (Screen 7, Step 4)                                                                                                                                                               | **Step 4's sub-heading promises something the product does not do, and contradicts the same PRD that specifies it.** The live copy reads "Your work is saved automatically **as you type**" (`application-step4-draft.tsx`, both funder-type branches). Saving is not keystroke-driven: `handleAnswerChange` only updates local React state and marks the answer dirty; the writes happen on **blur** (`handleAnswerBlur`) and via a **60-second background sweep**. There is no debounce timer and no `beforeunload` guard anywhere in the codebase. A user typing steadily for 59 seconds who closes the tab without clicking away loses that work. **The behaviour is correct — the copy is not.** `AC-FR-18-03` explicitly accepts "at most 60 seconds of edits potentially lost", and `PRD-Grant-Pathway.md`'s own FR-18 implementation notes describe the blur + sweep mechanism accurately, including a **2026-07-13 correction that specifically disproved a "400ms-debounced auto-save as the user pauses typing" claim**. That correction fixed the implementation note and never checked the user-facing string specified in §7 of the same document, which still says "as you type" — **the fix-the-instance-not-the-class pattern this project has now recorded seven times.** So this is PRD-versus-PRD, not merely code-versus-copy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Medium   | ⚠️ **Approved, not built.** Resolved by the same replacement string as `GAP-42`, which drops "as you type" entirely: **"Your answers are saved automatically. Run under `P5.2` with the anon key plus real user JWTs, per the two traps this row named. **Grant:** A can INSERT, SELECT and DELETE its own `<A_id>_<ts>` object. **Deny:** A cannot INSERT under B's id; B cannot SELECT or DELETE A's object; and **`<A_id>x…` with no underscore separator is denied**, confirming the trailing underscore that stops one user id which is a string prefix of another from matching. ⚠️ **A fixture defect in the harness had to be found first, and it matters:** the first run used a `text/plain` upload, which the bucket's `allowed_mime_types` (PDF/DOCX, `ADR-FILE-002`) rejects **before RLS is consulted** — so the deny assertions were passing for the wrong reason and proved nothing. Re-run with a real PDF fixture. A security test that passes for the wrong reason is worse than one that fails. These policies remain **defence in depth, not the primary control** (all real access is service-role behind a prefix check, as `ADR-FILE-001` accepts); what has changed is that the tripwire is now known to work. ⚠️ Dev only — re-confirm on prod at `P5.4`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| 🟢 Built       | GAP-50 | ADR-OPS-004, NFR-04                                                                                                                                                                                         | **The startup check that exists to stop the app booting on bad configuration let the bad configuration through.** `lib/env.ts` is imported by `instrumentation.ts` so the process throws before serving a request if anything is missing. On 2026-08-07 `AWS_REGION=` — name present, **value empty** — passed it, and cost most of a session. It slipped two guards that each look sufficient: the rule was `z.string().default('eu-west-2')`, and **a Zod default only fires on `undefined`** — an empty string satisfies `z.string()` and is returned unchanged; then both AI routes do `process.env.AWS_REGION ?? 'eu-west-2'`, and **`??` likewise only fires on null/undefined, never on `''`**. So the empty region reached the AWS request signer, every Bedrock call returned `403 signature does not match`, and the app booted reporting itself perfectly healthy. **Not a one-off:** `APP_VERSION ?? 'dev'` in `lib/version.ts` carries the identical defect, found while fixing the first — which is what turned this from a stricter rule on one variable into a fix for the class. Raised by WJ's decision after the author flagged it twice.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Medium   | **Normalise blanks before anything reads them.** New `lib/env-vars.ts` holds `APP_ENV_VARS` (the 18 variables the app reads) and `normaliseBlankEnvVars()`, which deletes any set to an empty or whitespace-only value, so **"set to nothing" and "not set" mean the same thing everywhere** — `.default()` fires, `??` fires, and a blank secret fails `.min(1)` loudly instead of being signed with. Run first in `validateEnv()`, before parsing. **Scoped to a declared list, not all of `process.env`**, because deleting arbitrary empty variables could affect Node, Next or the host OS. Extracted to its own module because `lib/env.ts` throws on import by design and therefore cannot be imported by a test — the same reason `lib/inactivity.ts` and `lib/docx-text.ts` were extracted. **Whitespace-only is treated as blank too:** a stray space from a copy-paste satisfies `.min(1)` and would be signed with. Locally that blocks only cron testing. **Production is fine on the record available** — `P5.4` carries a ✅ line confirming `CRON_SECRET` was added to Vercel with all three crons active — so this is a re-confirm at `P5.4`, not an incident. All three are now observed in Axiom with `status 200` and user agent `vercel-cron/1.0` — Vercel's own scheduler, which supplies the secret itself: `/api/cron/cleanup-guidelines` 2026-08-16 13:00:48 UTC (after that day's second `CRON_SECRET` rotation), `/api/cron/inactivity-warning` 2026-08-17 08:00:04 UTC, `/api/cron/inactivity-deletion` 2026-08-17 09:01:18 UTC. **The first 200 already made a strong inference** — all three routes read the same variable and compare it identically — **and it was still recorded as two-of-three unverified for a day rather than counted as passed.** The wait cost nothing and the inference held. ⚠️ **Axiom renders `_time` in local time while `vercel.json`'s schedules are UTC**, which briefly read as a missing 08:00 run; see `P5.4-RUNBOOK.md` step 4 for the note and the query.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

An application exported without them is incomplete. **Raised on WJ's ruling** that a project budget question the funder has actually asked is never excluded — which `lib/prompts.ts` has said since 2026-07-27; what it lacked was anything covering a _group_ of such asks. **This was first written up as a contradiction between `GCM-06`'s step 7 and the prompt, and that was wrong.** Step 7's "§5's expenditure/income table totals" means the `TOTAL EXPENDITURE =` footer **inside** §5a's grid — correctly excluded, since §5a's breakdown instruction is already its own card and the footer sums cells belonging to it — while §5b's three lines sit **below** the grid as standalone asks. Two different things; the observation conflated them. **Two suspected mechanisms, and the rule has to answer both:** three labelled currency lines read as a table summary, and every other thing the prompt says about totals is about excluding them; and `AMOUNT REQUESTED £` **also appears in the form's front administrative details table**, which is correctly excluded — so the exclusion appears to have attached to the _name_ rather than the _field_ and travelled to the budget section's own ask. **Fourth missing money question** (Idlewild Defect Log #1, MK Community Foundation, `GAP-39`'s budget half, now this) but **not a fourth instance of the same failure**: those three were about whether a budget question counts as a question, and their fixes were followed here for the figure that reads like a question. And explicitly: **excluding an administrative copy of a figure never licenses excluding the budget section's own ask for it** — exclude the front table's `AMOUNT REQUESTED` box, extract the budget question. The other side is stated in the same breath, because a rule that only said "extract money figures" would start extracting table footers: **the arithmetic footer of an itemised costs grid the applicant fills in themselves stays excluded**, since the breakdown instruction above it is already a question. `is_budget_question: true` keeps AI assist blocked on all three — these are the applicant's own figures and the model must not be invited to invent them. `GCM-06`'s step 7 reworded so it cannot be misread as covering §5b again.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-07 by a live re-run — `GCM-07` Pass.** §5 produced four cards: card 9 (§5a's itemised breakdown) and cards 10, 11, 12 — "Total needed for this project £", "Amount requested from SSTC £", "Balance outstanding £" — each Budget-tagged, separately worded, none merged. A rule that merely said "extract money figures" would have flattened it and produced a spurious card. `GCM-01`–`GCM-06` did not regress, which mattered: a rule loosened to stop dropping money fields is exactly what starts extracting contact details instead. | 🟢 Built       | GAP-52 | ADR-AI-007, ADR-AI-004, ADR-OPS-005, NFR-01                                                                                                                                                                 | **Step 3 was running about forty tokens under a hard output ceiling, and nothing anywhere said so.** `SUMMARY_MAX_TOKENS` in `app/api/generate-summary/route.ts` was 4000, with a comment describing it as comfortable headroom. It was not. On 2026-08-07 `GAP-51`'s new rule asked the model for two more budget cards, the response overflowed, and Step 3 began failing with **"We couldn't generate your summary right now. This is usually temporary — please try again"** — untrue on both counts. **The failure was invisible in three separate ways.** (1) The route logged only `input + output` combined, and `ai_usage_log` stores only that same combined figure, so the one number that mattered — how much of it was output — was recorded nowhere; establishing the cause meant reconstructing it across two days of usage rows. (2) `stop_reason` was present on every Bedrock response the entire time and was never read; it says `max_tokens` outright. (3) A truncation was classified as a **parse failure** and retried with the identical ceiling, which cannot produce a shorter answer — so one click spent two Bedrock calls to fail the same way twice, and then offered the user a button for a third. **Confirmed by measurement, not inference:** working back from a clamped 4000 predicted an input of 7636 tokens, and the run after the fix reported exactly `7636 in + 4210/6000 out`. 4210 is over the old ceiling; subtracting the ~340 tokens `GAP-51`'s rule added to the prompt puts the morning's successful outputs at ~3877 and ~3958 against 4000. **`ADR-AI-007` is a contributing condition:** it governs guideline **input** truncation at 150,000 characters and has no output-side counterpart at all, so the ceiling that actually broke the feature existed only as a constant in one file, named in no document.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | High     | **Raise the ceiling, and make the next one visible before it bites.** `SUMMARY_MAX_TOKENS` 4000 → **6000**. Not higher: generation ran at ~130 tokens/sec, so 6000 is ~46s and fits inside the existing `AbortSignal.timeout(60_000)`, while 8000 would be ~62s and would trade a parse failure for a timeout — going beyond 6000 means raising that abort and `maxDuration` too, which is a latency decision in its own right and not a side-effect of this one. **The log line now reads `7636 in + 4210/6000 out = 11846 tokens, stop_reason: end_turn`** — input and output separated, the output count printed against its own ceiling so proximity is legible at a glance, and `stop_reason` beside it. **A truncation no longer retries.** `stop_reason === 'max_tokens'` is checked **before parsing** — deliberately, because truncated JSON occasionally parses into a valid-looking object that is silently missing questions, which is worse than a visible failure — and returns immediately, refunding the AI slot and tagging Sentry with `ai_error: 'response_too_long'`. **New `response_too_long` error code**, distinct from `parse_error`: both yield unparseable JSON but they are opposite problems, since a parse error may clear on a retry and a truncation never will. Its message says the limit is ours and points at support, and **offers no retry** — Step 3 gets a dedicated `too-long` state with no "Try again" button, because the old one was a loop with no exit.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | ✅ **Cap raise and diagnostics verified live 2026-08-07; the safety net verified 2026-08-14, pulled forward on WJ's request.** The 6000 ceiling and the new log line were both confirmed on the `GCM-07` re-run — `7636 in + 4210/6000 out = 11846 tokens, stop_reason: end_turn`, 200 in 38.6s — which is what let `GAP-51` and `GCM-07` close. **The guard itself stayed unverified for a week because nothing in real usage had exceeded 6000 tokens since the raise.** `response-truncation.test.ts`'s 17 tests are a source scan, not a call to the route — proving the guard exists in the right shape, not that it executes correctly. **Closed 2026-08-14, pulled forward on WJ's request, with a genuine integration test rather than waiting for a real document large enough to force a truncation naturally:** `__tests__/generate-summary-truncation-guard.test.ts` mocks Bedrock's response (not the network, not the model) to report `stop_reason: 'max_tokens'`, calls the real `POST` handler with Supabase/rate-limit/Sentry all mocked, and confirms every step of the guard actually executes — `response_too_long` returned (not `parse_error`), exactly one Bedrock call (no doomed retry), `cancel_ai_slot` called with the right log/user IDs, and `Sentry.captureException` tagged `ai_error: 'response_too_long'`. A control-case test (a clean `end_turn` response through the same harness) confirms the mocks aren't accidentally forcing the truncation branch on every input. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, all 274 tests pass (269 → 274). **Two related items remain deliberately not fixed, unchanged by this test:** `REFINE_MAX_TOKENS` is **800** in `app/api/refine-answer/route.ts`, and a 500-word answer is ~670 tokens before the JSON wrapper and any warning prefix — the same class of problem, a different route, and it has no `stop_reason` check either. And `ADR-AI-007` still has no output-side truncation policy — raising a constant is not a documented position on what happens when a form is too big to summarise in one call. Both remain WJ's to rule on.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |

Activating the skip link's `href="#main-content"` moved the URL fragment and scrolled the viewport, but `document.activeElement` fell back to `<body>`, not the `<main>` element — confirmed live via direct DOM inspection, not inferred. Chromium's own "sequential focus navigation starting point" fallback meant the very next Tab press still landed correctly on the first focusable element inside `main` (confirmed on both layouts, and re-confirmed after the `+ New Application` → Step 1 client-side transition, which resets focus to `<body>` exactly as a fresh page load does — closing the "focus order after navigation" question AC-03 had flagged for this case), so sighted keyboard users were never actually blocked. But no landmark was ever really focused, which is the standard gap WCAG technique G1 exists to close: assistive tech gets no confirmation the skip worked, and the correct behaviour was resting entirely on a browser fallback rather than the page itself. **Fixed same day**: added `tabIndex={-1}` (plus `outline-none`, since a programmatically-focused non-interactive container doesn't need a visible ring) to both `<main id="main-content">` elements. Verified live on both layouts — `document.activeElement` is now the `<main>` element itself immediately on activation — and `axe-core` still reports zero violations on both a public and an authenticated page after the change. | 🟢 Built       | GAP-66 | ADR-OPS-006                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **The charity-lookup search field's own label was announced twice by NVDA, and it likely wasn't the only place.** Found 2026-08-10 live during AC-07 Step 3 — WJ's Speech Viewer transcript showed "Search by charity name or registration number" read out twice when tabbing into the field on `/profile`. Source inspection of `components/contextual-tooltip.tsx` found the cause: `wrapInStableSpan` (used here specifically to avoid reopening `GAP-36`, which needed a wrapper around native inputs to stop Base UI's `Tooltip.Trigger` from setting `type`/`name` props that reinitialise and blur a real `<input>`) renders `<span tabIndex={0}>` around the input unconditionally. The wrapped `<input>` is already natively focusable, so the span's own tab stop is redundant — and it's a documented Chromium quirk that a focusable, nameless generic wrapper's computed accessible name can pick up a nameless descendant control's own label, producing the double announcement. `wrapInStableSpan` has exactly one live usage today (`charity-profile-form.tsx`'s charity-lookup field); the sibling `active={!allApproved}` path (`application-step4-draft.tsx`'s "Ready to assemble" tooltip) uses the same span but for a different, legitimate reason — a _disabled_ Button drops out of the tab order entirely, so the span's `tabIndex={0}` there is the only way a keyboard user can reach the tooltip at all. | 🟢 Built       | GAP-79 | `design-requirements` §8.6                                                                                                                                                                                    | **Text sized in `px` does not respond to the browser's default font-size preference — only to zoom — contradicting §8.6's own requirement to "use `rem`... to respect user browser font size preferences".** Found 2026-08-12, live, during AC-15's Step 4 check (the plan's own text flagged this as likely before testing began): overriding the root element's font size directly (`html { font-size: 32px }`, doubling the 16px default — the actual mechanism a browser's font-size preference changes) and re-measuring every sampled text node on Step 4 showed zero change in any computed `font-size`. **Scope, re-verified against the live codebase 2026-08-12 (same day, later):** 384 `text-[Npx]` font-size instances across 30 component/page files, confirmed via `grep -c` (a stray leftover git worktree at `.claude/worktrees/` — already on record as a fully-committed 2026-08-06 leftover, see the AC-14 note above — was double-counting the same files and excluded). No `leading-[Npx]` line-height instances exist (line-height already uses Tailwind's `rem`-based keyword classes throughout). The 384 collapse into only **11 distinct pixel values**, with `14px`/`13px`/`15px`/`12px` covering roughly 90% — the same substitution repeated, not 384 independent decisions. Concentrated in `application-step4-draft.tsx` (69), `application-step3-summary.tsx` (35), `charity-profile-form.tsx` (31), `register-form.tsx` and `dashboard-populated.tsx` (23 each), `application-step5-approve.tsx` (22). | Large     | **Decision made 2026-08-12 (same day, later): convert the 384 instances to `rem`, not amend §8.6.** WJ's reasoning: some users rely specifically on the browser's font-size preference rather than zoom (zoom forces whole-viewport reflow/horizontal scrolling many of those users are trying to avoid), so `px` + zoom is not an equivalent substitute — amending the spec would mean formally accepting a real, live AA gap, against `ADR-OPS-006`. **Revised effort estimate: around half a day** — build an 11-row `px`→`rem` lookup table, script the substitution across the 30 files (mechanical, same pattern repeated, no logic changes), re-run AC-15's Step 4 check to confirm it now responds, spot-check a few screens. Smaller than this row's "Large" size classification suggests — that sizing is about instance/file-count breadth, not difficulty. Tasked as `P5.3b` item 6 in `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md`. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-13.** Re-verified the scope was unchanged before building (still 384/30/11, exact match). Re-derived the lookup table against the app's unmodified 16px root (confirmed via `app/globals.css`'s `html { @apply font-sans; }`, no `font-size` override) — `11px→0.6875rem` through `28px→1.75rem`, all exact fractions, no rounding. Scripted the substitution (regex `text-\[(\d+)px\]`, which also correctly matches the two `focus:text-[…]` variant-prefixed and one template-literal-embedded instance found) across the 30 files — 384 replaced, 0 `px` matches remaining, 0 unknown values. Deliberately used arbitrary `rem` values rather than Tailwind's named scale classes (`text-sm` etc.), since each named class carries its own paired `line-height` that would have silently overridden the already-correct, independently-set `leading-*` classes throughout — this way only `font-size` changes. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0` and all 266 tests pass (no logic touched, pure className strings). **Live-verified** by reusing AC-15's own repro: doubled the root font size (`document.documentElement.style.fontSize = '32px'`) on `/` and `/register` — sampled `rem`-converted elements now scale correctly (e.g. a `text-[0.875rem]` link went `14px → 28px`; the Register `<h1>` went `22px → 44px`), where before the fix nothing moved. Reset and re-sampled at the default size — every checked value matched its pre-conversion pixel value exactly (`14px`, `22px`, `16px`, `13px`, `15px`), confirming zero visual regression. Closes the sole open gap blocking AC-15; full detail in `accessibility-test-plan.md`'s AC-15 write-up. |
| 🟢 Built       | GAP-80 | ADR-OPS-006                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **`tt-charity-lookup`'s tooltip never announced to NVDA — GAP-69's shared fix put `aria-describedby` on the wrong element.** Found 2026-08-12, live, during `AC-08`'s formal tooltip sweep: tabbing to the Profile page's Charity Commission search input, NVDA read only the input's own label, no tooltip content. Root cause: this is the one `ContextualTooltip` usage wrapping a native form control (`wrapInStableSpan`, needed per `GAP-36` to stop Base UI resetting the input's `type`), which puts `aria-describedby` on the wrapping `<span>` — but focus lands on the `<input>` inside it, not the span, so the description is attached to an element nobody is ever focused on. | Medium   | **Generate the tooltip's id in `ContextualTooltip` itself (not `Tooltip`'s own internal one) and clone it onto the actual child control whenever the span wrapper is in use**, so the description follows whichever element really receives focus. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-12, same day found.** `components/ui/tooltip.tsx`'s `Tooltip` now accepts an `id` override; `contextual-tooltip.tsx` generates one `React.useId()` and clones `aria-describedby` onto the wrapped child. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0` and all 266 tests pass. **Live-verified**: WJ tabbed back to the same input and confirmed NVDA now announces the tooltip content. |
| 🟢 Built       | GAP-81 | ADR-OPS-006                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **`tt-ready-to-assemble`'s tooltip never announced to NVDA either, for a different reason than `GAP-80`.** Found 2026-08-12, live, in the same sweep: tabbing to the disabled "Ready to assemble" button, NVDA read the disabled button's own role and state ("clickable... button, unavailable") but never the tooltip's explanatory text. Unlike `GAP-80`, the wrapping `<span>` genuinely is the focus target here (it gets `tabIndex={0}` specifically because a disabled native `<button>` can't receive focus at all) — but NVDA still reported the nested disabled button's own role/state rather than the span's `aria-describedby`, live-confirmed with the description simply absent either way. | Medium   | **Clone the tooltip id onto the wrapped child unconditionally whenever the span wrapper is used**, not only when the child (not the span) is the literal focus target — covering whichever element the screen reader ends up describing, rather than relying on which one the DOM says is focused. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-12, same day found, same fix as `GAP-80`.** The `GAP-80` fix already covers this once the "only clone when the span isn't the focus target" condition was removed — `aria-describedby` is now cloned onto the child in both span-wrapper shapes. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0` and all 266 tests pass. **Live-verified**: WJ tabbed back to the same button and confirmed NVDA now announces both the button's state and the tooltip content. |
| 🟢 Built       | GAP-82 | ADR-OPS-006                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **`tt-budget-no-ai`'s tooltip never announced to NVDA — a third distinct failure shape from `GAP-80`/`81`.** Found 2026-08-12, live, in the same sweep: tabbing to the Step 4 budget-section warning (a bare `<div tabIndex={0}>` wrapping an icon and a `<p>`, composed directly onto `TooltipTrigger` with no span wrapper), NVDA read the visible warning paragraph but never the tooltip's separate hint text. Two hypotheses were tested live and ruled out before landing on the fix: giving the div an explicit `role="group"` had no effect, and a minimal same-shape reproduction outside the app confirmed the browser's own accessibility tree doesn't expose a "description" distinct from role/name for this experiment either way — `aria-describedby` is not reliably announced by NVDA on a role-less (`div`/`span`/`p`) trigger at all, regardless of role. | Medium   | **Append the tooltip text as a visually-hidden (`sr-only`) node inside the trigger itself, for role-less native tags only**, relying on name-from-content — which NVDA already reads correctly for these elements (it read the visible paragraph text the same way) — instead of `aria-describedby`. `h1`/`button`/`a`/`input` triggers are unaffected and keep using `aria-describedby` as before. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-12, same day found.** `contextual-tooltip.tsx` now detects a role-less native trigger tag (`div`/`span`/`p`) and appends the tooltip content as a `sr-only` span alongside the original content, instead of relying solely on `aria-describedby`. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0` and all 266 tests pass. **Live-verified**: WJ tabbed back to the budget warning and confirmed NVDA now announces both the static warning text and the tooltip content. The same fix likely also covers `application-step2-form.tsx`'s `<p tabIndex={0}>` trigger (same role-less shape) but that one is not yet independently live-checked — not part of `AC-08`'s required sample. |
