Source inspection of `application-step2-form.tsx` confirmed why — the progress bars themselves are correctly built (`role="progressbar"` with `aria-valuenow`/`aria-label`), but the adjacent `<p>Uploading…</p>` (line ~306) and `<p>Processing document…</p>` (line ~326) carry no `role="status"`/`aria-live`, so their appearance and any text change are invisible to assistive technology — identical in kind to `GAP-68`'s original Step 3 finding. | Low      | **Add `role="status"` to both paragraphs**, matching the convention `GAP-68` already established for this exact failure shape — no new design decision needed. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-13, same day found.** `role="status"` added to both `<p>` elements in `application-step2-form.tsx`. `type-check` passes. **Not yet live-verified** — small test files upload/process too fast in practice to confirm the announcement lands audibly; carried forward as an open confirmation, not assumed fine, pending a slower/larger test file or artificially throttled network. |
| 🟢 Built       | GAP-85 | ADR-OPS-006                                                                                                                                                                                                 | **Picking a file orphans focus for a moment, and NVDA announces "unknown."** Found 2026-08-13, live, during `AC-09`'s Speech Viewer capture: right after selecting a file, the log showed "unknown" followed by a repeated document-boundary announcement, before the filename was read. Root cause: `application-step2-form.tsx`'s dropzone (`role="button" tabIndex={0}`, the element focused when the file picker was triggered) is conditionally rendered only in the `idle` state — the instant `processFile()` sets `uploadState` to `'uploading'`, that focused DOM node is removed, and focus falls back to something the browser/AT can't identify before recovering. The code is still genuinely unguarded (no `isRedirectError`-style check exists), but whatever makes the throw actually reach this component's `catch` is evidently intermittent, not unconditional — plausibly a race between `startTransition`'s handling of the manually-awaited action call and the `NEXT_REDIRECT` signal arriving (this action is invoked via a plain `await` inside `useTransition`, not via `<form action={...}>`, which is the one pattern the bundled Next.js docs show working transparently), or one of the two genuinely intermittent causes `lib/action-error.ts`'s own header comment names this message for in the first place (version skew from a mid-session deployment, or session-timing edge cases). Left uncorrected in the row's earlier prose above as the point-in-time record; the true claim is "the code is unsafe and has been observed to misfire," not "it always misfires." **Likely not isolated to this one button**: `actions/applications.ts` has the identical "success path ends in `redirect()`" shape in `createApplication`, `saveApplicationStep1`, `advanceToStep3`, `advanceToStep4`, and `assembleAndAdvance` — any client call site wrapping one of these in the same catch-all pattern would show the same false alarm. No `isRedirectError`/`unstable_rethrow`-style guard exists anywhere in the codebase (grep-confirmed) to distinguish a redirect throw from a real one. | Medium   | **Detect and re-throw the redirect signal instead of swallowing it as a generic failure** — check the thrown value's `digest` for the `NEXT_REDIRECT` prefix (or use whatever helper the bundled Next.js docs currently recommend for this exact case — check `01-app/02-guides/server-actions.md` before implementing, per `AGENTS.md` §1, since this is a newer Next.js version than training data covers) and only show `ACTION_FAILED_MESSAGE` for genuine non-redirect failures. Worth auditing the other five actions sharing this shape for the same false-alarm risk while in the area. **Research finding (2026-08-14, later, WJ away from keyboard): the bundled `server-actions.md` guide states plainly that a `redirect()` call inside a Server Action is carried in the *same* single-roundtrip response as a success — "the response navigates the router and streams the destination's RSC Payload" — not as a distinct error condition the client has to specially unwrap.** That guide also names the one class of failure this exact scenario is actually vulnerable to: action IDs rotate on new deployments (at most every 14 days, even with no source change) and a client on an older build can invoke an ID the current deployment no longer recognises — surfacing as "Failed to find Server Action" — which is precisely the "version skew" cause `lib/action-error.ts`'s own header comment already names `ACTION_FAILED_MESSAGE` for. **Reproduced locally to test this directly**: ran a throwaway account through this exact flow (paste-guideline funder, 3 questions, approve, "Ready to assemble") three times in a row against the local dev server, reading the Network panel each time. All three produced a clean `303 See Other` on the `step/4` POST and landed on the senior-review screen with no error banner and no console error — consistent with the docs' description, and inconsistent with the code path being inherently unsafe under ordinary conditions. **This narrows, not closes, the gap**: the code is still genuinely unguarded against a real "Failed to find Server Action" response being misreported as a plain transport failure (which is arguably correct messaging anyway, since `ACTION_FAILED_MESSAGE` already covers that case reasonably) — but the practical risk looks like the documented version-skew/session-timing class the message was built for, not a bug that fires on an arbitrary fraction of ordinary clicks. **Checked directly, not left open: no production deployment landed anywhere near the 2026-08-14 MKCF-08 observation** — `vercel ls --prod` shows nothing between roughly 18 hours before that point and this session's own docs-only commits afterward, so a mid-session redeploy (and the action-ID rotation the docs tie to it) is ruled out as the cause of the original occurrence. That leaves a genuine one-off network hiccup as the most likely explanation for what was actually seen — precisely the ordinary case `ACTION_FAILED_MESSAGE`'s copy is already written to handle gracefully, not evidence of a recurring code defect. Worth WJ deciding whether this still warrants the redirect-digest guard as a hardening measure, or whether it's better read as the message doing its job once, correctly, on a rare blip. **Decision (2026-08-14): build it.** Not a probability argument — `unstable_rethrow` (`next/navigation`) is the officially documented API for exactly this case, and the bundled docs name `redirect()` explicitly as one of the errors that "should be rethrown and handled by Next.js itself," not caught as an application error. Swallowing that signal is incorrect code regardless of how often it manifests, the fix is small and mechanical, and this project ships multiple production deployments a day (today alone triggered several) — the action-ID rotation this exposes to is a real, recurring risk over time, not a one-off. **Scope check before building, not assumed: the "likely shared by five other actions" claim above was wrong.** Read every call site of `createApplication`, `saveApplicationStep1`, `advanceToStep3`, `advanceToStep4`, and `assembleAndAdvance` across the codebase — none of them wrap the call in a `try/catch` at all (two have an explicit comment acknowledging `redirect()` "never returns" on success), and `createApplication` is invoked via `<form action={...}>`, the one pattern the docs show working transparently. Only **2 catch blocks in 1 file** — both in `application-step4-draft.tsx`, both calling `setDraftReadyToAssemble` (`handleReadyToAssemble` and the inner catch inside `handleManualContinue`) — were actually at risk. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-14.** Added `import { unstable_rethrow } from 'next/navigation'` and called it at the top of both affected `catch` blocks in `application-step4-draft.tsx`, before the existing `ACTION_FAILED_MESSAGE` handling — a genuine redirect now passes through to Next.js's own machinery instead of being caught, while a real transport failure still shows the existing message. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, all 269 tests pass. **Live-verified**: reproduced the full flow against the local dev server (throwaway account) — clean `303 See Other`, correct landing on the senior-review screen, no false error banner, no regression versus the pre-fix behaviour. Test account and application deleted after verification. |

| 🟢 Built | GAP-107 | ADR-OPS-005, ADR-OPS-002 | **Sentry's `environment` tag cannot distinguish production from preview deployments, and `P5.4` is about to build two alert rules that assume it can.** Found 2026-08-16 answering `G1` of the parity checklist. All three runtime configs set `environment: process.env.NODE_ENV` (`sentry.client.config.ts:7`, `sentry.server.config.ts:7`, `sentry.edge.config.ts:7`). **On Vercel, `NODE_ENV` is `production` for preview builds as well as production ones** — Next.js sets it at build time and Vercel builds previews in production mode — so every deployed environment reports the same tag. Local development reports `development`, so the tag does separate local from deployed; **what it cannot separate is the distinction the alerting depends on.** `VERCEL_ENV` (`production` \| `preview` \| `development`) carries it and is not used, though it is already present in the environment — it appears in `.env.local`. **The same expression also drives `tracesSampleRate` (`0.1` when `NODE_ENV === 'production'`), so preview deployments are sampled at the production rate as well** — a smaller consequence of the same root cause, and one that quietly consumes the Sentry quota this project is on. **This is the `GAP-103` pattern in the observability layer**: a signal that cannot tell two environments apart, relied on as though it could. | Medium | **Set `environment: process.env.VERCEL_ENV ?? process.env.NODE_ENV`** in all three Sentry configs — the fallback keeps local development reading `development`, where `VERCEL_ENV` is unset. Consider the same expression for `tracesSampleRate` so previews are not sampled at the production rate. ⚠️ **Sequence this before `P5.4` step 13**, which creates the new-issue alert rule and `GAP-03`'s P95 performance alert. Both are specified as production-scoped, and on today's tagging preview traffic satisfies that scope — building them first means building them on an axis that does not yet mean what the rule says. **Existing Sentry issues carry the old tag**, so anything retained from before the change stays ambiguous; note the changeover date rather than back-filling. | 🔴 **OPEN — raised 2026-08-16, no covering task.** Found by reading the three configs while answering `G1`, not from an observed mis-tagged event — **the claim about `NODE_ENV` on Vercel preview builds is from the platform's documented behaviour, and the cheapest confirmation is to read the `environment` tag on any existing issue from a preview deployment before changing anything.** Raised rather than fixed on the spot because it changes what production alerting means, and `P5.4` step 13 is the task that consumes it. 🔵 **BUILT 2026-08-16, same day, at WJ's instruction — server and edge are fixed; the client leg needs one Vercel setting before it takes effect.** New `lib/sentry-environment.ts` resolves the tag once for all three runtimes and is imported by each config, which also serves the "three-file rule" comment those files carry better than three copies of an expression would. ⚠️ **Two corrections to this row's own Action text, both material.** **(1) `tracesSampleRate` was deliberately left on `NODE_ENV`, against what this row originally recommended.** Keying it to the new value would have raised preview deployments from 10% to **100%** sampling — the recommendation would have _increased_ quota use while claiming to reduce it. The split that matters for sampling is local-versus-deployed, which `NODE_ENV` already expresses correctly; `GAP-107` is about the tag, not the rate. **(2) `||`, not `??`.** `.env.local` carries `VERCEL_ENV=` — set with a blank value — and `??` falls through only on null/undefined, so the proposed `??` would have tagged every local event with an empty string. **That is `GAP-50`'s failure mode exactly**, reproduced in the fix for a different gap; caught before commit by reading `.env.local` rather than assuming the variable was absent. ⚠️ **Outstanding — the client half is inert until Vercel's "Automatically expose System Environment Variables" is enabled on the project.** Non-public variables are not available in the browser at all (confirmed in the bundled Next.js docs `01-app/02-guides/environment-variables.md`, per `AGENTS.md` §1), so the browser needs `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV`; `.env.local` carries `VERCEL_ENV` but **not** the public copy, which indicates the toggle is off. Until it is on, browser events keep the old imprecise tag while server and edge events are correct — **a mixed state that is worse to misread than the original**, so it is recorded here rather than left to be discovered. **Verified in the built client bundle, not inferred:** a clean `next build` shows the fallback chain present and reaching Sentry's init. **Side-finding worth keeping:** `@sentry/nextjs` already defaults `environment` to `` `vercel-${NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV}` `` when none is passed — visible in the same bundle — so passing nothing was a viable alternative fix. The explicit value was kept because it avoids the `vercel-` prefix and preserves `development` locally. **Also added:** `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV` and `VERCEL_ENV` to `lib/env-vars.ts`'s inventory — **`__tests__/env-blank-handling.test.ts` failed on the first run without them**, which is `GAP-50`'s staleness guard working as designed. `type-check`, `lint --max-warnings 0`, **280 tests** and a clean `next build` from an empty `.next` all pass. ✅ **VERIFIED and closed 2026-08-16 — and the earlier "waiting on a Vercel toggle" note above was wrong.** Vercel's **"Enable access to System Environment Variables"** was **already on**; the inference that it was off came from `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV` being absent from `.env.local`, which turns out not to indicate the project setting at all. Recorded rather than quietly deleted, because it is the second inference in two days drawn from a local file about a remote environment. **Two pieces of evidence, and the second is the one that proves it.** (1) The **live production client** was read from the running site — `environment: "production"`, `release: 0cdbca8…` (this commit), DSN set — which confirms the deployment but **proves nothing on its own**, since production reports `production` under the old code too. (2) **A clean build with `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV=preview` inlines `environment:"preview"` as a literal into the client chunk** — the whole fallback chain folded at build time — which is the discriminating result. ⚠️ **A live preview deployment could not be used**, and the attempt is what found **`GAP-108`**: a throwaway branch built green and then returned a server error, so no client Sentry ever loaded. **The one assumption remaining is Vercel's own documented behaviour** — that it sets `NEXT_PUBLIC_VERCEL_ENV` to `preview` on preview builds. The code is proven to do the right thing with whatever value it is given; the platform supplying `preview` is taken on documentation, and is the thing to re-check if a preview ever tags wrongly. ✅ **That last assumption is now discharged too, 2026-08-16, by an event nobody planted.** Sentry issue `GRANT-PATHWAY-A` ("Connection closed.", event `b3659ce3`) is tagged **`environment: preview`** with `url: https://grant-pathway-git-gap107-rapidglobes-projects.vercel.app/` — a **real browser event from the throwaway preview**, proving the platform supplies `preview` and the client reads it. **Under the old code this event would have been tagged `production`**, and a production-scoped alert rule would have fired on it. **Nothing about `GAP-107` now rests on documentation or on a build artefact.** ⚠️ **The event is also the browser half of `GAP-108`** — the server threw at startup, so React's streaming connection died mid-page and the client's global handler reported the closure. **Two gaps, one event, neither of them where the error message pointed.** |
| 🔴 No task | GAP-106 | ADR-SEC-006, FR-02 | **Every Supabase password rejection renders one message, and that message is self-contradictory for a breach-list rejection.** Found 2026-08-16 by reading the code while preparing `GAP-104`'s verification test. `actions/auth.ts` maps Supabase's `weak_password` error onto a single state, and all three forms render one fixed sentence for it — _"Your password must be at least 12 characters and include both letters and numbers"_ (`components/register-form.tsx:107`, `components/reset-password-form.tsx:135`, `components/account-settings-form.tsx:69`). Supabase returns `weak_password` for **three** distinct causes — `@supabase/auth-js` types them explicitly as `["length", "characters", "pwned"]` — and the third only becomes reachable once `password_hibp_enabled` is on. **A breached password typically satisfies both conditions the message states.** `Password123456` is 14 characters with letters and digits; a user rejected for it is told to do something they have already done, with no path forward except guessing. **The mapping was correct when it was written** — until 2026-08-16 the leaked-password check was off in production, so length and characters were genuinely the only causes, and `lib/validation.ts` caught both client-side first. Turning `GAP-104`'s toggle on is what makes the message reachable in a state where it is wrong. Latent on dev since 2026-06-29 (`VQ-009`), live on production from 2026-08-16. | Medium | **Distinguish the breach case from the rule case in the message.** Supabase's `AuthWeakPasswordError` carries a `reasons` array (verified in `node_modules/@supabase/auth-js`, not assumed); where it contains `pwned`, render a message saying the password has appeared in a known data breach and a different one should be chosen — the rule sentence stays for genuine length/character failures. Applies to all three forms, which share the state but not the copy. ⚠️ **Do not resolve this by softening the message into something vague enough to cover both** — the reason a user cannot act on it today is precisely that it says nothing true about their password. **Wording is a `PDR`-class product decision, not a developer's choice**, and NCSC guidance on breach-rejection wording is worth reading before drafting it. | 🔴 **OPEN — raised 2026-08-16, no covering task.** Raised rather than fixed on the spot because the fix is user-facing copy on the registration path and `P5.1` closed with the current wording reviewed. **Not yet observed live** — predicted from the code and from `GAP-104`'s settings change, and the `P5.5` test written for `GAP-104` will produce exactly this message as a by-product, which is the cheapest possible confirmation. Sequence it before `P5.5`'s flagship runs if it is to be fixed, since three test plans exercise registration. |

| ✅ Closed  | GAP-102 | ADR-OPS-003, C13 | **The published Privacy Policy places Resend in the United States; Resend's own console shows this domain sending from Ireland (eu-west-1).** Found 2026-08-15 during `P5.4`. `privacy-policy-external.md` lists Resend's location as "United States" and states "Two of our service providers are based outside the United Kingdom — Resend and Vercel (both United States)", relying on IDTA safeguards. The sending region for `grantpathway.org.uk` is **Ireland**, which is EEA. **`ADR-OPS-003` names no region at all**, so nothing in the document set records where transactional email is actually processed. **The discrepancy runs in the safe direction** — the policy claims weaker protection than reality, so no user is misled into thinking their data is better protected than it is — and it may be deliberate, since Resend is a US **company** and US entity access is possible regardless of sending region. But `P5.1` closed with an independent solicitor review, and a reviewed legal document now carries a detail that does not match the console. | Low | **A decision, not an edit.** Establish whether "United States" was chosen to describe the corporate entity (in which case add the infrastructure region for accuracy) or the infrastructure (in which case it is wrong and should say Ireland/EEA). Then record the region in `ADR-OPS-003`, which is silent on it. Re-check whether the solicitor review needs revisiting or whether this is below that threshold. | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-17 — premise disproven, no change required. The published policy was right and the finding was wrong.** Resolved by reading Resend's own terms rather than its console. **Resend's DPA states plainly that personal data is transferred to and processed in the United States as necessary to provide the service**, and offers **no data-residency or region-selection option at all**; its sub-processor list is **22 entities, every one of them United States** (AWS, Cloudflare, PlanetScale, Snowflake, Datadog, Vercel, Stripe, Supabase and others). The legal entity is **Plus Five Five, Inc.** **The `eu-west-1` reading was the _sending_ region — where mail leaves from — not where data is processed or stored.** Two different things, and the console shows only the first. **v1.6's "United States" was correct all along.** ⚠️ **This gap nearly made the policy worse.** The v1.7 draft had moved Resend to "Ireland (European Union)" and rewritten Section 6 around it, which would have claimed EEA processing for a squarely US processor — a published document less accurate than the one it replaced. Caught only because WJ asked what Resend's own data-protection statements say. **The lesson is specific and worth keeping: a vendor console shows infrastructure, a DPA states processing. For a privacy policy, the DPA governs.** **Transfer basis confirmed lawful and now named in the policy:** EU SCCs Module Two, UK SCCs with the UK Addendum, and the EU–US Data Privacy Framework with its UK Extension. **Provider substitution considered and declined (WJ, 2026-08-17).** EU-owned alternatives exist (Scaleway TEM, Brevo, AhaSend, Lettermint) and Amazon SES `eu-west-2` would satisfy `C13` while reusing the existing AWS DPA — but Supabase, Vercel and AWS all have US parents, so changing email alone would improve one table row without changing the jurisdiction position. Recorded as a post-launch question. ⚠️ **One thing found in passing and NOT closed by this row:** Resend's sub-processors include **Anthropic, PBC ("Artificial Intelligence")** and **RunPod ("Self hosted LLMs")**, while the policy states "We do not use your information to train artificial intelligence models". True of what Grant Pathway does; unverified through Resend. Worth its own check before that sentence is relied on. |

| 🟢 Built | GAP-100 | ADR-SEC-006, ADR-OPS-003, ADR-STACK-004 | **`grant-pathway-prod`'s Supabase auth was configured to redirect to a domain owned by somebody else.** Found 2026-08-15 during `P5.4`'s Supabase walkthrough. It is not on this Vercel project: the Domains list shows only `grant-pathway-three.vercel.app` as valid. **Three distinct failures, all live in the configuration at once:** (1) our real host `grant-pathway-three.vercel.app` was **not allowlisted at all**, so `emailRedirectTo` would be rejected and Supabase would fall back to Site URL — meaning **every production registration would deliver the user's verification token to the third party's website**; (2) password reset uses `${origin}/auth/callback?next=reset`, and an **exact-match** allowlist entry does not match a query string — Supabase's own docs confirm `*` stops at `.` and `/` separators while `**` does not — so reset was broken independently of the host problem; (3) neither `grantpathway.org.uk` nor `www.` was listed, guaranteeing total failure at the `P5.6` DNS cutover. **The reset failure was engineered to be silent:** `requestPasswordReset` is deliberately fire-and-forget (`actions/auth.ts`, "we do not check the error because we must never reveal whether the email address is registered" — `AC-FR-05-02`), correct for enumeration resistance but it means a rejected redirect reaches neither the user nor Sentry. | **High** (impact if launched) / **no exposure to date** | Replace the Site URL with the real pre-launch host and allowlist `/auth/callback**` for every host that can serve the app. **Scope to the callback path, not `/**`** — Supabase's guidance prefers exact paths in production, and this is the narrowest pattern that still matches the query string. | ✅ **BUILT 2026-08-15**, by WJ during the walkthrough, verified by screenshot. Site URL → `https://grant-pathway-three.vercel.app`; the third-party entry **deleted**; three entries added — `grant-pathway-three.vercel.app`, `grantpathway.org.uk` and `www.grantpathway.org.uk`, each `/auth/callback**`. **Severity assessed rather than assumed, and it cuts both ways.** The attack variant is **blocked**: Next.js Server Actions compare `Origin` to `Host` and abort on mismatch (verified in the bundled `data-security.md`), so a forged origin cannot make the server emit a redirect to the foreign host. But the **normal-use** variant needed no attacker at all — ordinary registration would have leaked tokens on day one. **Zero actual exposure**, because production has never had a real user and DNS has never been cut over. ⚠️ **`P5.6` must change Site URL to `https://grantpathway.org.uk`** at cutover; recorded in `P5.4-RUNBOOK.md`. **Why it survived this long:** production auth had never once been exercised — every "live-verified" result in this project's history was obtained against `grant-pathway-dev`, which is exactly the gap `P5.5` exists to close. |
| 🟢 Built | GAP-99 | ADR-SEC-005, ADR-SEC-003 | **No application-level rate limiting on the authentication actions.** Found 2026-08-15 during `P5.2`'s OWASP pass. `lib/rate-limit.ts` defines two Upstash limiters and both are correctly applied — `aiRatelimit` (5/60s) on `/api/generate-summary`, `/api/refine-answer` and the charity lookup; `resendRatelimit` (3/hour) on `resendVerificationEmail`. **`signIn`, `registerUser` and `requestPasswordReset` carry no application-level limit** and rely entirely on Supabase Auth's built-in limits. That is a real control, but **this project has never verified what it actually is**: `supabase/config.toml`'s `[auth.rate_limit] email_sent = 2` governs the **local development** instance only, not either hosted project. Note user enumeration is separately and correctly prevented — `requestPasswordReset` returns `sent` unconditionally, deliberately and with a comment saying so. | Low–Medium | **Do not reflexively add an app-level limit to sign-in.** Too tight and a legitimate charity is locked out of its own grant application, which for this product is a worse outcome than the attack it mitigates — a product judgement, not a security one. Instead, at `P5.4`: read and record the auth rate limits actually configured on `grant-pathway-prod` (Authentication → Rate Limits). | ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-15 — the figures have now been read and recorded, which was the whole deliverable.** `grant-pathway-prod` → Authentication → Rate Limits, read directly from the dashboard: **sign-ups and sign-ins 30 per 5 minutes per IP (360/hour)**; **token verifications 30 per 5 minutes (360/hour)**; token refreshes 150 per 5 minutes (1,800/hour); emails **30/hour**; anonymous sign-ins 30/hour; SMS 30/hour. **IP address forwarding is OFF.** **Assessment: adequate, and no application-level limit is warranted.** 360 sign-in attempts per hour per IP is a meaningful brute-force control, and the reasoning for not adding an app-side limit stands unchanged — too tight a limit locks a legitimate charity out of its own grant application, which is a product judgement rather than a security one. **Two caveats recorded rather than glossed:** the limits are **per IP**, so they do not constrain a distributed attempt against one account; and **email at 30/hour is a shared ceiling across verification, reset and change-email**, which is comfortable at current volumes but is the number to revisit if registrations ever arrive in bursts. Originally: ⚪ assessed and accepted 2026-08-15 — "the gap is that nobody has looked". Somebody has now looked. _Superseded note: closes when the prod figures are read at `P5.4`, alongside the `GOTRUE_SECURITY_UPDATE_PASSWORD_REQUIRE_CURRENT_PASSWORD` reconciliation already listed there. |

GAP-20 remains open (P5.1 task). **⚠️ Annotation added 2026-07-30 (Phase 5 restructure item C4) — the sign-off is not being rewritten, but it must not be read at face value. Of the nine gaps listed above as "resolved", only GAP-26 was built at the time; the rest were _tasked_ — a step written into `IMPLEMENTATION-PLAN.md` and nothing more (see the status-symbol key at the top of this document, which is why 📋 now exists). The statement was true as written and false as read. As at 2026-07-30, GAP-24 is built (export disclaimer, commit `a3f21f9`) and five remain unbuilt — GAP-05, GAP-21, GAP-22, GAP-23 and GAP-25, all verified by direct inspection that day and now carried by the new `P5.3b`. GAP-12 (v1.0.0 tag) and GAP-17 (cross-user RLS test) are also still outstanding, in P5.6 and P5.2 respectively. Nothing here invalidates the Phase 4 → Phase 5 transition itself; the gate's purpose was to confirm nothing was unaccounted for, and nothing was. What it did not confirm — and was never worded to confirm — is that the code existed.** Privacy policy Section 7 corrected — 7-day backup disclosure was missing from the live file (docs/legal/ was updated but app was serving a stale duplicate at docs/; consolidated to docs/legal/ as single source). Monthly AI cap confirmed at 50 across all three AI routes (generate-summary, generate-draft, refine-answer); generate-draft was incorrectly at 20 and corrected.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | GAP-11 (blocked — GitHub Pro required), GAP-20 (open — P5.1 licence review)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | ✅ Signed off — WJ, 2026-06-17                                                                                                                                                        |
| Phase 5 → Phase 6 | 2026-08-06 | **Added retrospectively, and it gated nothing.** Phase 6 began on 2026-07-05 and P6.1–P6.5 were already built when this row was written; it records the state of the register as found on 2026-08-06, not a decision taken before Phase 6 started. All 47 register rows were walked and each Resolved cell read directly: **31 ✅ built · 4 partial (GAP-27, GAP-39, GAP-40, GAP-48) · 8 📋 tasked-only · 4 ➖ accepted deviation · 0 ⚠️**. Four things surfaced that only a full pass can surface. **(1) `GAP-32` is not in the Gaps register at all.** That register's own preamble says "All ⚠️ rows consolidated here for easy triage"; `GAP-32` (`ADR-DATA-002` — rewording `FR-22` and `AC-FR-22-01/02/03` after the "never store guidelines" reversal) has sat as ⚠️ in the ADR consequence table since 2026-07-10 with a Task cell reading "Not yet scheduled to a task", and was never copied across. It is invisible to every register-based triage, and it is the only number between 01 and 48 with no register row — which matters past triage, because the register is also where a new session looks for the next free GAP number. A number source that omits live numbers is the same class of hazard that let two parallel sessions each issue `GAP-39` and `GAP-40` on 2026-08-06. **(2) The two halves of this document disagree about `GAP-01`, `GAP-02` and `GAP-04`** — still ⚠️ Gap in the `ADR-AI-003`, `ADR-AI-005` and `ADR-AI-009` consequence rows, recorded as built on 2026-05-21 in the register. This is the split the 2026-07-30 status-symbol note was written about, running the other way: `AGENTS.md` §2 sends every session to the ADR consequence tables, so a stale ⚠️ there buys wasted re-work as reliably as a stale ✅ there buys a missing feature. **(3) The Task column still points at tasks the 2026-07-30 Phase 5 restructure moved.** `GAP-21`, `GAP-22` and `GAP-23` read `P5.3`, but the restructure created `P5.3b` to hold the spec deviations and the Go-Live gate lists all three under `P5.3b`. `GAP-12` reads `P5.4`, but the `v1.0.0` tag was explicitly moved to `P5.6` that same day, "which would have tagged the release before it was live". Following the register's own pointers today leads to the wrong task. **(4) `GAP-27` carries no status symbol** — its cell reads the bare word "Partial". It predates the 2026-07-30 key and was not among the sixteen rows reclassified that day. **All four were fixed later the same day, on WJ's instruction, and this row is left as written rather than rewritten** so the finding and the fix stay legible as separate events: `GAP-32` was added to the register, which now holds **48 rows — numbers 01–48 complete, each used exactly once — tallying 31 🟢 built · 8 🟡 tasked-only · 4 🔵 partial · 4 ⚪ accepted · 1 🔴 no task**, the 🔴 being `GAP-32` itself, which is why the counts above show none; `GAP-01`/`GAP-02`/`GAP-04` were corrected to ✅ in the ADR consequence tables after verifying all three in the code; the Task column was re-pointed for `GAP-12` (→ `P5.6`) and `GAP-21`/`GAP-22`/`GAP-23` (→ `P5.3b`); and `GAP-27` was given the 🔵 symbol it never had. The register was also sorted into numeric order and given a colour-coded Status column. | **8 📋, all unbuilt work still owed:** GAP-03, GAP-28 (P5.4), GAP-05 (P5.3), GAP-17 (P5.2), GAP-21, GAP-22, GAP-23 (P5.3b), GAP-12 (P5.6). **4 partial:** GAP-27 (Sentry performance monitoring → P5.4), GAP-39 and GAP-40 (prompt rules rewritten 2026-08-06; only a live `GCM-06` re-run closes them), GAP-48 (migration written and replaying in CI, not yet applied to either Supabase project). **1 unscheduled:** GAP-32. | ⬜ **Not signed — drafted 2026-08-06, awaiting WJ.** A retrospective gate cannot be signed as though it had run on time, and nothing here should be read as clearing Phase 6's entry. |
